                                                                                Tristate Holdings Limited Annual Report 2025   3
Chairman’s Statement



                                               WANG Kin Chung, Peter
                                       Chairman and Chief Executive Officer




Our Business
I am pleased to present to shareholders the annual results for the
year ended 31 December 2025 of Tristate Holdings Limited. For
the year under review, the Group reported revenue of HK$3,626
million and profit attributable to equity shareholders of HK$125
million, compared to HK$4,184 million and HK$156 million,
respectively, in the prior year. Included in the 2025 profit is a non-
cash gain of HK$123 million arising from the early termination of
the Reebok licence right. Excluding this one-off gain, the Group’s
2025 net profit declined relative to last year, primarily attributable
to decreased revenue and profit from our garment manufacturing
business.

During the year, our garment manufacturing business experienced
declined revenue and profit, caused by decrease in demand from
certain customers amid a challenging global trade environment
and intensified competition. To mitigate the impact on our
performance, the Group continued implementing measures to
enhance production efficiency and competitiveness through
automation and streamlined operations. Despite the challenges to
our garment manufacturing business in 2025, we saw increased
orders for our Vietnam factory. In response to customers’ growing
demand, we have further expanded our Vietnam manufacturing                    Reebok’s revenue fell by 32% in 2025 compared to last year and

the performance of our existing physical and digital stores,
improving operational efficiency and increasing customer lifetime
value.

For our licensed brands business, we remain positive on the
outdoor segment notwithstanding the overall challenging
consumer environment in China. For Nautica and Spyder, we
will continue to elevate brand image, expand new product lines
and categories, improve sell-through and enhance supply chain
performance. We will also maintain disciplined control over
operating costs. Despite persistent market headwinds, we see clear
growth opportunities in second-tier provinces.

Notes:

1.      Under HKFRS 16, the Group as a lessee is required to recognise interest expense accrued on the outstanding balance of the lease liability and the depreciation
        on the right-of-use assets, instead of recognising rental expenses incurred under operating leases on a straight-line basis over the lease term. In the cash flow
        statement, the Group as a lessee is required to classify rentals paid under the capitalised leases as financing cash outflows.

2.      Licence related amortisation and imputed interest on licence fees payable are non-cash items recognised in accordance with the accounting policy for our long-
        term licences – Nautica, Spyder and Reebok.

3.      ROE is calculated as profit attributable to equity shareholders over average total equity for the current and prior year.

Principal Risks and Uncertainties
The Group has an enterprise risk management mechanism in place to identify, evaluate and manage its exposure to risks (including
environmental, social and governance risks). Management oversees the risks and implements robust business processes to mitigate the
risks. Existing and emerging risks are identified, evaluated and tracked regularly by the management and reported to the Audit Committee.

Principal risks and uncertainties affecting the Group are outlined below:

     External Risks                                     Operational Risks                              Financial Risks

     Regulatory Risks                                   IT Risks

Operational Risks

Increased Cost

•   Increased cost will impact the profitability of our business.      •   For our brands business, we have our own sourcing team with
                                                                           diversified supply network to handle product sourcing.

                                                                       •   For our garment manufacturing business, the Group earns
                                                                           cut and make profit. Increased cost in fabric material has little
                                                                           impact on the Group.

                                                                       •   Diversification of factories and supply chain in various
                                                                           countries in Asia and production process improvements will
                                                                           help offset the rise in wages and staff costs.

•   Failure to comply with applicable laws, regulations or standards   •   The Group manages internal controls over our significant

    adversely impact our employees, lose production time and               of resources utilisation and reduce environmental emissions in
    attract negative media attention and regulators’ interest.             our business operations.

IT Risks

•   When there is IT system outage or cyberattack, all the IT          •   Appropriate controls and technology have been deployed
    systems may come to a halt causing not only business                   to mitigate the risk of system outage and cyberattack. They
    interruption but also loss of confidential information such as         include preventive system maintenance, regular security
    personal data of employees or consumers of the e-shops.                checks, installation of firewall and anti-virus software, multi-

                                                                           recovery drill, assignment of job-related access rights and
                                                                           well-defined access controls system.

                                                                       •   Although certain e-shops are run on third party platform, the
                                                                           e-commerce service agreement specifies that the operator

                                                                           necessary to guarantee the proper functioning of the e-shops,
                                                                           the safety of the systems underlying the e-shops and the
                                                                           protection of personal data according to applicable laws and
                                                                           market practices.

16   Tristate Holdings Limited Annual Report 2025
Corporate Governance Report


Board Process
The Board holds regular Board meetings four times a year roughly at quarterly intervals and ad hoc Board meetings as and when required.
Dates of regular Board meetings are scheduled one year in advance to facilitate Directors’ attendance. In addition, notice of at least fourteen
days is given for each regular Board meeting and reasonable notice is given for each ad hoc Board meeting. All Directors are given the
opportunity to propose agenda item. Those Directors who are not able to join physically at a meeting may participate through video-
conferencing or telephone conferencing.

For regular Board meetings, and as far as practicable for ad hoc Board meetings and other meetings of the Board committees, an agenda
accompanying meeting papers is sent to the Directors and the relevant Board committee members at least three days before the intended
meeting date.

Minutes of meetings of the Board and the Board committees are kept by the company secretary of the Company. Draft minutes of
meetings are circulated to the Directors/Board committee members for comment within a reasonable time after the meetings are held.
Final/signed versions of minutes of meetings are circulated to all Directors for information and records.

1. Message from the Chairman
Tristate Holdings Limited (the “Company” or “Tristate”, and together with its subsidiaries collectively the “Group”) is committed to pursuing
sustainable development across all business units and delivering sustainable value to our stakeholders. Over the years, the Group has
embedded environmental, social and governance (“ESG”) principles throughout our operations.

We aspire to be a leader in responsible garment manufacturing and an outstanding brands operator. To realise this vision, we advance
responsible production across our value chain and uphold the upmost sustainability standards of our global customers. We are committed
to complying with regulatory requirements and fostering a sustainable apparel industry.

As we execute and expand our ESG initiatives, we will continue to strengthen our sustainability capabilities and advance our business
towards a sustainable future.

2. About this Report
2.1 Overview
This is the ESG report of the Group for the year ended 31 December 2025 (“this Report”).

2.2 About Our Business
Founded in 1937, Tristate is a globally recognised manufacturer of premium fashion apparel famous for its unrivalled ability to integrate
cutting-edge technologies into innovative fashion production. The Group’s principal business activities comprise garment manufacturing
and brands business, serving customers across Europe, North America and Asia. As a brands business operator, the Group manages
different in-house and licensed brands, one of which is our own C.P. Company as covered by this Report.

2.3 Reporting Period
This Report adopted the reporting period from 1 January 2025 to 31 December 2025 (the “reporting year”), which is in alignment with the
reporting period of the Company’s annual report for the year ended 31 December 2025.

2.4 Scope of this Report
The scope of this Report covers: (i) the Group’s garment manufacturing business and operations, including facilities in Hefei, Panyu, Thailand,
Vietnam and Myanmar; (ii) the Group’s own brand C.P. Company which is mainly operated in Europe; and (iii) our Hong Kong head office
operations.

2.5 Reporting Reference
This Report has been prepared in accordance with the Environmental, Social and Governance Reporting Code set out in Appendix C2 to
the Rules Governing the Listing of Securities on The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited (the “Stock Exchange ESG Reporting Code”).
This Report will focus on the environmental and social issues. For the corporate governance policies and practices adopted by the Group
for the reporting year, please refer to the “Corporate Governance Report” on pages 13 to 24 of this annual report.

This Report has been prepared in accordance with the following four reporting principles, which are aligned with the Stock Exchange ESG
Reporting Code:

3. Our Value and Missions
3.1 Our Vision
Our core principle is to deliver quality products and services to our customers ethically and responsibly. As a responsible garment
manufacturer and brands business operator, Tristate is committed to fostering a sustainable environment through the continuous
reduction of carbon emissions and production waste, and efficient use of energy.

As a third-party manufacturer for internationally renowned apparel brands and the manufacturer and distributor of our branded products,
we are dedicated to advancing sustainable development throughout our value chain. Tristate believes that a corporation must act
responsibly to drive meaningful change and ensure business operations are economically, socially, and environmentally sustainable.

Our vision is to foster a truly sustainable apparel industry while fulfilling our commitment as a corporate citizen. In running our business, we
adhere to ethical practices by balancing the needs of our shareholders, customers, employees, the community and the environment.

3.1.1        Corporate Citizenship
We are committed to corporate citizenship by meeting the legal, ethical and economic responsibilities expected by our stakeholders. Our
goal is to enhance living standards and quality of life in the communities where we operate, benefiting all stakeholders.

We have established a clear sustainability vision and mission for the Group. Accordingly, we develop policies and initiatives to ensure strict
regulatory compliance and actively promote employees’ professional and personal development.

The Group believes transparency and accountability are essential to building stakeholders’ trust. We are committed to broadening
stakeholder engagement to better understand their needs and expectations. We improve transparency of our community involvement
through different effective corporate communication channels. We also encourage management to integrate corporate citizenship
considerations into our business decisions and operations.

3.2 Our Missions
The following are three sustainability missions that we pursue with our stakeholders:

Facilitating Responsible Production Along the Value Chain
To provide quality products, resources consumption and emissions are inherent in garment production. As a responsible manufacturer, we
prioritise reduction of environmental impact of our production facilities.

Our garment manufacturing customers are international apparel brands with rigorous sustainability expectations for both their operations
and supply chains. As a key supply chain partner, we play a vital role in ensuring our manufacturing process and products meet the
sustainability standards of our customers, thereby helping us shape our sustainability goals. With a view to procuring positive feedback from
regular client audits and site visits, we ensure our compliance with local environmental standards and continually monitor and improve our
resources efficiency, which led to the Group’s contribution to sustainable development of the apparel industry.

Promoting Equality and Fairness
As a global garment manufacturer and brands operator with different production facilities and offices and thousands of employees across
multiple regions, we recognise the significant impact we may have on local communities. Therefore, we place great emphasis on our
responsibilities as a corporate citizen and maintain good business practices.

We promote equality and fairness by providing employees with stable employment, fair compensation, and improved living standards. We
provide safe working environment in our production facilities, prohibit child and forced labour, and promote business ethnicity.

Promising Quality and Safe Products
As a trusted manufacturer for globally recognised high-end fashion brands and as a qualified brands operator, we are committed to
providing consumers with safe and high-quality apparels.

From a consumer perspective, garment lifecycle spans from purchase to end-of-life disposal. By enhancing product quality and
craftsmanship, we can extend lifespan and reduce waste throughout the value chain.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report


4. Maintaining Robust Governance
4.1 ESG Governance Structure
The Board has the overall responsibility for the Group’s ESG strategies and reporting. The Board discusses and formulates strategies
on material ESG issues, oversees ESG risks and opportunities and sets ESG performance targets. The Board takes into account ESG
considerations in strategic planning, major investment decisions and risk management processes and policies. When considering ESG
issues and setting applicable ESG targets, the Board pays specific attention to climate-related topics, particularly climate-related risks and
opportunities of the Group. The Board also reviews and approves the annual ESG report. To ensure Board members are kept abreast of the
latest ESG rules and regulations, the Company provides annual training to Board members covering applicable ESG (including climate-
related) training topics. All Board members had received such annual training in the reporting year.

Under the oversight of the Board, the ESG steering committee (the “ESG Committee”) is established as a management committee to
take responsibilities in executing the ESG strategies and climate-related directives approved by the Board. The ESG Committee is tasked
with overseeing the implementation of ESG policies, monitoring day-to-day ESG matters, formulating recommendations for ESG initiatives,
proposing and implementing ESG targets, and ensuring adherence to applicable legal and regulatory requirements. In carrying out its
foregoing ESG duties, the ESG Committee will give due considerations to climate-related policies, matters, initiatives, targets and legal and
regulatory requirements.

Supported by the Group Sustainability Manager, and general managers and relevant staff across business units, the ESG Committee also
closely monitors and keeps tracking the Group’s ESG (including climate-related) performance against targets, identifies and assesses ESG
risks and opportunities (including climate-related ones), and responding to emerging ESG trends and stakeholder expectations.

Material ESG issues identified by the ESG Committee are reported to the Board annually, including the submission of the ESG report to the
Board for approval.

While climate-related factors are not currently a formal component of the Group’s remuneration structure, the Group realises the increasing
importance of ESG performance to its long-term growth and will consider enhancing our incentive practices by factoring into our
remuneration policies the relevant ESG (including climate-related) performance metrics in appropriate time.

4.4 Business Ethics and Anti-corruption
Tristate upholds the highest standards of integrity, honesty and fairness in business practices and enforces the same standards across
all levels of employees. The Group maintains a zero-tolerance policy against corruption or fraudulent behaviours. We are committed to
full compliance with all applicable laws and regulations regarding anti-corruption, including but not limited to the Prevention of Bribery
Ordinance (Chapter 201 of the Laws of Hong Kong).

Our code of conduct (the “Code of Conduct”), complemented by our staff handbook, provides employees with clear guidelines regarding
acceptance of gift, management of conflict of interest and reporting concerns. These guidelines strengthen employees’ understanding
of bribery prevention and enable them to identify potential immoral acts or violations. To remind employees to adhere to the Code of
Conduct at all times, we circulate the Code of Conduct annually to all managers and employees in sensitive positions to refresh their
knowledge, and these employees are required to submit annual conflict of interest declaration. The Group provides training, e-learning and
awareness programmes on anti-corruption to all employees. During the reporting year, training on anti-corruption was provided to the
employees and the Company’s directors.

To foster a corruption-free culture and encourage misconduct reporting, the Group has established a whistleblowing policy (the
“Whistleblowing Policy”) with documented procedures. This policy provides a confidential escalation channel – including a dedicated
email address and hotline – for internal and external stakeholders to lodge complaints about misconduct, malpractice or irregularities.
Every report is assessed by our Internal Audit Department and investigated accordingly. Where necessary, cases are referred to the Audit
Committee or reported to relevant regulatory bodies. Whistleblowers’ identities are kept confidential to protect them against any retaliation.
The Audit Committee holds overall responsibility for the implementation, monitoring, and periodic review of the Whistleblowing Policy
to ensure its effectiveness. For details, please refer to the Whistleblowing Policy and the Code of Conduct published on the website of the
Company (www.tristateww.com).

Furthermore, to address potential conflict of interest that could interfere with employees’ ability to make independent decisions regarding
purchase and procurement, the Group has established its procurement policy. Any supplier violating this policy will be terminated and
immediately removed from our list of suppliers, while employees concerned will be subject to appropriate disciplinary actions including
termination of employment.

During the reporting year, the Group was not aware of any material non-compliance with laws and regulations regarding anti-corruption.

5. Caring for Our People and the Community
Being a responsible corporate citizen, the Group has always been concerned about the welfare of the communities where we operate.
Our business operations provide employment, learning, and development opportunities that contribute to local economic growth and
sustainability of the communities. We maintain ethical workplace practices and prohibit unethical conduct and unlawful labour. We actively
encourage employees to engage in community services to support sustainable local development.

5.1 Labour Practices and Human Rights
People are the Group’s most valuable asset. We believe transparent recruitment practices and competitive remuneration are essential
for recruiting and retaining high-calibre employees, thereby contributing to the Group’s long-term development and success. Our
employment practices comply with relevant laws and regulations of the regions where we operate, including the Employment Ordinance
(Chapter 57 of the Laws of Hong Kong) and the Labour Law of the People’s Republic of China. During the reporting year, we were not aware
of any material non-compliance with laws and regulations regarding employment and labour practices by the Group.

5.1.1        Labour Standards
The Group complies with applicable labour laws and regulations to prohibit the employment of child or forced labour in our operations.
During the recruitment process, identification documents of applicants are checked to ensure no underage worker will be hired.

We respect employee rights and prohibit any form of unfair treatment. The Group has established a grievance policy that provides a formal
mechanism for employees to raise their concerns with management. All employees work on a voluntary basis and can resign in accordance
with their employment contracts. The Group complies with applicable local statutory maximum number of working hours, and overtime
work is accepted on a voluntary basis with compensation in accordance with relevant legal requirements.

During the reporting year, there was no employment nor use of child or forced labour by the Group, and we were not aware of any material
non-compliance with laws and regulations regarding labour standards by the Group.

5.1.2        Employment and Labour Practices
Recruitment and Promotion
In our recruitment and selection process, hiring decisions and salary packages are based on consistent, objective and job-related criteria,
including experience and qualifications, to ensure fairness and equal opportunity. Staff rewards and promotions are determined by their
relevant merits and performance through annual appraisal. Further details of the Group’s diversity and inclusion principles are provided in
sub-section 5.4 of this Report.




30   Tristate Holdings Limited Annual Report 2025
Environmental, Social and Governance Report


Workplace Flexibility
The Group supports workplace flexibility to enhance employee well-being, work-life balance and operational efficiency.

We provide flexible working hours in our Hong Kong head office to foster a culture of trust, accountability and balance between
professional and personal priorities. In addition, smart working is available for C.P. Company for applicable roles, subject to local collective
bargaining agreements. To further support diverse needs, C.P. Company also offers part-time arrangements for study purposes, as well as
during maternity leave periods and post-maternity transitions.

Encouraging Communications
Employees’ feedback drives continuous business improvement. We encourage management and employees to maintain open dialogues
and two-way communications. Employees are encouraged to discuss any issues of concern with their immediate supervisors or, if
needed, escalate issues to the management team. Employees may also raise their concerns on an anonymous basis through the Group’s
whistleblowing system.

Benefits Entitlement
We offer competitive remuneration and welfare packages to attract, motivate and retain talents. Employee performance is assessed
through annual appraisal, with salary adjustment based on individual contribution and the Group’s financial performance. Outstanding
performers may receive discretionary bonuses and/or share options. All employees enjoy benefits including retirement plans, paid leave,
medical insurance, and other welfare provisions, fully compliant with applicable labour laws and regulations.

5.2 Health and Safety
The health and safety of our employees is our top priority in all our operations. Our garment manufacturing processes are primarily
conducted indoors, involving cutting, sewing, finishing and packaging tasks. Although our processes have low occupational safety risk, we
maintain regular checks on our facilities and equipment to ensure compliance with applicable occupational health and safety legislation.

To provide our employees with a safe and healthy working environment, we manage our workplaces in compliance with applicable
occupational health and safety legislation. Our working areas are adequately lit with proper ventilation, along with basic necessities like
potable water, sanitary facilities, fire protection devices and first-aid kits.

To ensure the safety of our working environment, regular evaluation of our production and working areas is performed to identify and
mitigate any potential safety hazards with appropriate control measures. Safety measures include periodic machinery check, physical and
chemical hazards identification and control, facility hygiene control, emergency preparedness for accident and incident, safety training for
employees, and tracking of health and safety statistics. We record lost days and incident data to identify improvement opportunities.

Regular safety training for employees is performed to increase safety awareness and minimise workplace hazards aiming to prevent
fatalities, severe work-related injuries and significant errors related to failure of equipment or facilities. Fire drill, emergency response
practice, fire extinguisher demonstration and workplace hazard workshop are organised annually to prepare employees for emergency
response. Workers in our production lines are required to wear protective gears at all times to ensure health and safety.

During the reporting year, no severe work-related injuries were recorded, and we were not aware of any material non-compliance with laws
and regulations regarding occupational health and safety by the Group.

5.3 Human Capital Development
Investment in human capital is vital to the Group’s success. Through appraisal process, we not only evaluate performance but also
identify training needs that support career development. Complemented by orientation sessions and on-the-job training, we equip our
employees with up-to-date industry knowledge and expertise to strengthen employees’ capabilities, thereby enhancing the overall market
competitiveness of the Group.

During the reporting year, the Group provided training that covered industry knowledge, technology and product knowledge, quality
standards, work safety protocols and communication skills. In addition, C.P. Company offered comprehensive management training
programmes focusing on communication, management by objectives, team selection and labour management to enhance leadership
capabilities. The Group continually reviews employees’ training needs and supports their career growth through education sponsorships for
job-related courses.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report


5.4 Diversity and Inclusion
Tristate is an equal opportunity employer that treats all employees equitably through an “even-hand” approach. The Group promotes fair
competition and prohibits discrimination or harassment against any employee in all aspects. To foster a supportive and just workplace that
values diversity and in response to the latest regulatory requirement, the Group has adopted a workforce diversity policy in the reporting
year. This policy emphasises equal employment opportunities for all employees regardless of age, gender, race, ethnicity, nationality, creed,
religion, family status, marital status, maternity condition, disability, cultural background and personal views. We prohibit discriminatory
conduct in all human resources activities, including recruitment, training, transfer, promotion and termination. By our Code of Conduct,
staff handbook, and employment application form, the Group establishes equal employment standards and prohibits discrimination and
biased decision-making. Please refer to the “Diversity across Workforce” in the “Corporate Governance Report“ of this annual report to further
understand the Group’s workforce diversity.

We have established procedures for reporting, investigating and resolving harassment complaints, with escalation options to line managers
or Human Resources Department and access to external assistance. Retaliation against complainants, alleged harassers or informants is
prohibited, and all parties must maintain confidentiality. We will progressively integrate diversity, equity, and inclusion considerations into
our internal processes, including recruitment, training and succession planning.

During the reporting year, there was no reported case relating to equal opportunity violations, discrimination or workplace harassment by
the Group.

5.5 Community Relations
We recognise that fostering community development in the areas where we operate is critical to the sustainable growth of our business.
Guided by our commitment to corporate social responsibility, we actively contribute to community welfare and engagement. Our
environmental, social and governance policy (the “ESG Policy”) has outlined our specific commitment for community investment.

During the reporting year, we donated to The Salvation Army for its charitable activities and participated in the Mid-Autumn Festival Appeal
2025 to support the purchase of daily supplies and foods for those in need. We also supported the elderly through the purchase of charity
cookies from Helping Hand. Sharing in the community’s sorrow over the Tai Po fire incident, we made emergency relief donations to those
affected.

Our C.P. Company in Europe is highly committed to supporting the communities in which it operates, with dedicated efforts to urban
regeneration and social inclusion projects in collation with local associations. In the reporting year, C.P. Company donated to the
Associazione Eleonora Cocchia Vivere a Colori ONLUS, an association in Italy which supports research and prevention of oncological
diseases. Aiming to leverage sport as a catalyst for community participation especially the youth, C.P. Company continued the sports
project - “There is No Us Without You” to promote the positive value of sport through collaboration with local sports association.
C.P. Company also partnered with City in The Community, the beneficial foundation of Manchester City football club, to restore a deprived
area in Manchester, the UK.

The Group actively promotes volunteerism and civic engagement among employees. To support this, employees of C.P. Company are
encouraged to participate in company-organised charitable activities and may take one day of leave annually to volunteer with approved
non-profit organisations of their choice. Since 2015, we have been recognised by The Hong Kong Council of Social Service as a Caring
Company for our commitment to corporate social responsibility – caring for the community, employees and the environment.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report


6. Promising Quality and Safe Products
The Group promotes ethical, responsible and sustainable procurement practices. We are committed to managing our supply chain
in environmental-friendly and socially responsible manner and have implemented robust supply chain management and product
responsibility policies to ensure responsible practices along our supply chain.

6.1 Supply Chain Management
6.1.1  Supplier Selection Policy
Our customers, who are primarily international premium apparel brands, have high expectations on our supply chain. We work closely
with qualified suppliers to evaluate their performance based on capability, quality, compliance status, pricing and certifications, and ESG
performance. The ESG Policy outlined our commitment to integrating environmental and social considerations into our supplier selection
procedures. The Group assesses suppliers’ regulatory compliance with environmental, employment, labour, and occupational health and
safety standards. C.P. Company sets requirements for suppliers on product manufacturing, material use and traceability, product conformity,
and ethical business conduct. Only suppliers who comply with our requirements are eligible to partner with the Group.

We also encourage suppliers to prioritise environmental considerations in their production processes and uphold fair employment
practices. We have zero tolerance for suppliers’ unethical treatment and illegal labour practices, including child or forced labour and any
form of discrimination.

6.1.2     Sustainable Sourcing
Tristate recognises the importance of continuously enhancing our responsible sourcing practices, sustainable manufacturing and
environmental stewardship. We continue modernising our operational processes to improve efficiency and limit our environmental
impact. Our production facilities are certified by the Responsible Down Standard (“RDS”), Responsible Wool Standard (“RWS”), Sustainable
Regenerated Cellulosics Content Standard (“SRCCS”, formerly known as SVCoC), Global Recycled Standard (“GRS”), Organic Content
Standard (“OCS”) and Better Cotton Initiative (“BCI”).

RDS           The RDS is a leading standard for animal welfare in down and feather products to ensure the continuous improvement of
              best animal welfare practices in the down industry. The scope of the RDS includes the full own supply chain from the farms
              and slaughter facilities (animal welfare) to the down processors and garment factories (traceability).

RWS           The RWS is a voluntary global standard that addresses the welfare of sheep and of the land they graze on. The RWS provides
              verification of the practices that are happening at farm level, giving brands a clear solution that will allow them to make
              claims about their wool sourcing with confidence.

SRCCS         The SRCCS is a globally acknowledged, voluntary, and all-encompassing product standard. It sets forth the benchmarks for
              third-party authentication of sustainable regenerated cellulosic fibre content, chain of custody, social and environmental
              practices, risk assessment and safe chemical management practices.

GRS           The GRS is an international, voluntary and full product standard that sets the requirements for third-party certification of
              recycled content, chain of custody, social and environmental practices, and chemical restrictions.

OCS           The OCS is an international and voluntary standard that provides chain of custody verification for materials originating on a
              farm certified to recognised national organic standard. The standard is used to verify organically grown raw materials from
              the farm to the final product.

BCI           The BCI is the world’s leading sustainability initiative for cotton. Its mission is to help cotton communities survive and thrive,
              while protecting and restoring the environment.




                                                                                                          Tristate Holdings Limited Annual Report 2025   33
Environmental, Social and Governance Report


6.2 Product Responsibility
6.2.1   Product Quality and Safety
Product quality and safety are fundamental to the Group’s production processes. We strive to meet our customers’ expectations by
delivering products and services of high quality.

To ensure consistent and high-quality products, we source most of our raw materials from client-designated suppliers. The Group, working
collaboratively with our customers and suppliers, has established standard procedures to maintain and enhance product quality and safety.

The ESG Policy outlines our commitment to providing quality products that meet the Group’s requirements and clients’ expectations. Our
quality control systems, implemented in our facilities, ensure compliance with these standards. Our customers – primarily international
apparel brands – conduct regular factory visits and audits to monitor our compliance with ethical and technical standards. We maintain
active dialogue with our customers through satisfaction surveys to identify areas for improvement.

For C.P. Company, suppliers are required to implement quality and safety controls throughout production, including in-line and final
inspections, and conduct regular laboratory tests to validate compliance with the relevant technical and quality requirements.

The Group conducts product risk assessment during development and implements a pre-production process to ensure we deliver reliable,
safe and quality products that comply with relevant legislation. We follow an inline quality control process and apply AQL (Acceptable
Quality Level) standard for inspection of finished goods across all orders.

The Group is committed to delivering product fit for purpose. However, if any delivered product does not meet customer’s expectation, our
account manager will work with the responsible manufacturing unit to investigate the root cause and develop a corrective action plan for
improvement. For C.P. Company, when non-conformities are identified, suppliers must repair or replace the affected products at their own
costs, with re-inspection of the corrected items.

6.2.2        Data Security and Privacy Protection
We place great importance on safeguarding the confidentiality and privacy of customer data. Our Code of Conduct and staff handbook
contain clear guidelines for employees on how to handle confidential information. Employees are required to protect customer data and
are strictly prohibited from any unauthorised disclosure of customers’ data or trade secrets to third parties.

To reinforce our commitment, the Group reminds our employees periodically on data security and privacy through the request for annual
conflict of interest declarations. We have IT guidelines that address personal data protection and regulations governing the use of IT tools.
To strengthen cybersecurity awareness, employees receive educational emails on how to identify fake emails and participate in phishing
attack simulation. We have set out a clear escalation mechanism in our Code of Conduct and staff handbook to enable employees to
promptly report any data breach, phishing attempt or suspicious incident.

6.2.3        Responsible Marketing and Labelling
The Group follows responsible marketing practices by providing product information that reflects the characteristics of its garments. We
respect intellectual property rights and have established procedures to protect proprietary information and intellectual property of our
in-house brands.

6.2.4        Customer Feedback Channels
To ensure we stay connected with our customers, the Group provides multiple feedback channels, including telephone, email, survey
and face-to-face meeting. We highly value customers’ input, as it helps us identify opportunities for improvement and shape our business
strategies. Based on customers’ feedback, the Group develops and implements effective corrective action plans to address identified areas
of weakness.

During the reporting year, we were not aware of any material non-compliance with laws and regulations regarding product responsibility
by the Group.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report


7. Facilitating Green Production along the Value Chain
7.1 Our Commitment
We recognise our role in addressing global environmental challenges across our value chain. Beyond meeting regulatory requirements for
resources efficiency and emission controls, we seek opportunities to integrate enhanced environmental practices into our daily operations.

In 2021, with the objective of continually improving our environmental performance, we set the following reduction targets for 2025 and
have been regularly monitoring our performance against such targets. Our latest performance-to-target comparisons have been presented
the relevant sub-sections of this Report.

 Target Types                    Targets for 2025 (using 2021 as baseline year)         Sub-sections
 Energy                          Reduce energy intensity by 8%                          7.2.2 Energy and GHG Emissions
 Water                           Reduce water intensity by 5%                           7.3.1 Use of Water
 GHG emissions                   Reduce GHG intensity by 9%                             7.2.2 Energy and GHG Emissions

The environmental targets for 2025 (using 2021 as baseline year) were not fully achieved, mainly due to changes in production volume,
operational adjustments and external market conditions. The Group has reviewed the implementation progress and will strengthen
relevant management measures and set a new target as mentioned in the paragraph below.

To align with China’s dual carbon goals – achieving peak carbon by 2030 and carbon neutrality by 2060, we have reviewed our strategic
objectives and set a new emissions reduction target for the Group. Our focus remains on emissions reduction across key operational
areas in our production facilities and we aim to reduce greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions (Scope 1 and Scope 2) intensity by 9% by
2030, using 2025 as baseline year. The intensity target is calculated based on gross GHG emissions. The Group will monitor the progress
regularly. Through ongoing energy efficiency projects, we strive to steadily improve our utility consumptions. The specific measures we are
implementing to achieve the goal are detailed in the following parts of this Report.

We have reviewed our GHG emissions calculation methodology to enhance data accuracy and consistency and have re-calculated 2024
energy consumption and GHG emissions data to improve year-over-year comparison. A full breakdown of our environmental performance
for the reporting year is available in sub-section 7.2.2 of this Report.

In addition to our factory operations, our in-house brand, C.P. Company, has assessed carbon footprints at both corporate and product
levels annually and monitors supplier performance with the support of an independent third party. Additionally, the repair service offered
by C.P. Company for its clients, namely Longevity Hub, continues to help extend the lifespan of C.P. Company garments and thereby
lowering the environmental impacts per wear. Regarding its journey towards achieving B Corp Certificate, C.P. Company exceeded its
original target score of 60.9 points to attain 61.2 points in the preliminary assessment in the reporting year, as compared to 51.3 points last
year. For more details of the brand’s environmental performance and specific actions, please refer to the latest C.P. Company Responsibility
Report on its website (www.cpcompany.com).

7.2 Climate Change Adaptation
7.2.1   Climate-related Risks and Opportunities
In alignment with the evolving environmental landscape, we recognise that climate change poses potential impacts on the Group’s
operations and the wider community. The Group is committed to managing climate change risks that may impact our business.

To address these challenges, the Group has integrated climate-related risks and opportunities into the Enterprise Risk Management
framework (“ERM framework”) of the Group. This integration allows us to formulate appropriate procedures and measures to prevent or
minimise potential disruption caused by climate change while leveraging emerging opportunities.

For the purpose of climate-related risk assessment, the Group defines short-term as one year, medium-term as two to five years, and long-
term as six to ten years, based on its business cycles and strategic planning periods. The Group identifies and assesses climate-related risks
and opportunities by evaluating their likelihood of occurrence and potential impact on operations, financial position and performance,
covering both physical and transition risks. The identification and assessment of climate-related risks are based on internal operational
data, business characteristics, regulatory developments and industry reference information. Material climate-related risks are prioritised and
managed through the ERM framework, with regular monitoring and review by the management.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report

costs are integrated into our general operating costs. The potential climate-related risks and opportunities identified by the Group are

The Company has not yet quantified the financial effect of these specific impacts as this requires enhanced market data and measurement
frameworks to distinguish them from other business factors.
Based on the specific objectives outlined in the environmental policy under our ESG Policy, the Group has integrated climate considerations
into its supply chain and operational planning. In light of the Group’s current business scale and operational model, climate-related risks are
continuously monitored and managed within our existing risk management framework. Although a standalone climate-related transition
plan has not yet been established, practical energy conservation and emissions reduction measures have been embedded into our routine
operational management.
The environmental policy under our ESG Policy outlines specific objectives to mitigate climate change issues by adopting an environmentally
friendly approach in our business operations. These objectives include:
•        Encourage the use of renewable energy, low-carbon and energy-efficient products and materials across our properties and offices.
•        Monitor and regularly report progress in carbon emissions management and reduction.
•        Collaborate with business partners along our value chain to mitigate climate change impacts.
•        Monitor climate-related risks and opportunities, and respond to market and technological shifts, and regulatory and policy changes
         associated with climate change.
The Group is committed to an environmentally friendly approach that encourages the use of renewable energy, low-carbon technologies,
and energy-efficient products across our properties and offices. Furthermore, our factories in Panyu, Hefei and Vietnam have participated
in a client-led “Best Practice Available” programme, which focuses on reducing carbon emissions and water consumption. Additionally, the
Group monitors and regularly reports progress in carbon emissions management and reduction, while collaborating with business partners
across the value chain to mitigate climate change impacts.
The Group has conducted an insurance review to safeguard against physical risks. All our factories and our China offices are covered by
Property All Risks insurance, transferring the risk of potential loss caused by natural disasters.
Looking ahead, the Group will continue monitoring regulatory development to ensure compliance with the changing requirements.
Leveraging our established risk management processes and existing documentation, the Group is well-positioned to enhance its resilience
and adapt to climate-related risks over time.

In the reporting year, we continued to improve the energy efficiency of our factories through three key initiatives: (a) ongoing maintenance
and upgrade of equipment and facilities, (b) environmental data collection and monitoring, and (c) enhancement of employee awareness.

(a) Ongoing Maintenance and Upgrade of Equipment and Facilities
Improvement/Initiatives in the Reporting Year
•   The solar photovoltaic system at our Thailand factory operated throughout the reporting year, generated 782,508 kWh of electricity,
    which accounted for 29% of the factory’s total onsite electricity. This initiative resulted in a reduction of 326 tons of CO2e GHG
    emissions during the reporting year.

•      The rooftop solar installation at our Vietnam factory, completed in the previous year, generated 292,554kWh of electricity in 2025,
       contributing 16% of the factory’s total electricity consumption. The GHG emission has been reduced by 193 tons of CO2e during the
       reporting year.

•      The Group has been expanding renewable energy capacity with rooftop solar panels at the Hefei factory. The Group has fully funded
       this project, which has a projected installed capacity of 1.4 MW.

•      To optimise energy efficiency, the Hefei factory suspended the use of two electrical transformers (1,600 kVA and 1,000 kVA).

•      Our Hefei factory continued to be certified by ISO 14001 Environmental Management System, demonstrating adherence to
       international environmental standards.

•      We conducted annual third-party audits using the Higg Facility Environmental Module (Higg FEM) at our Panyu, Hefei, and Vietnam
       factories, assessing performance across key areas including environmental management systems, energy use, water use, wastewater,
       air emissions, waste management and chemical management.

Highlighted Initiatives Carried Out in Previous Years
•   Installed an insulation layer on the rooftop of our Panyu factory and roof greening at several of our factory buildings with full or partial
    vegetation cover, which reduced the air-conditioning demand for the upper levels and hence reduced the consumption of energy.

•      Replaced part of the step-motor powered equipment with server-motor powered models, reducing the idle energy consumption by
       step-motors and improving occupational safety of our workers.

•      Upgraded to Variable Speed Drive chillers to reduce annual energy consumption while maintaining reliable operation.

•      Installed solar lighting tubes at the ceiling of our Hefei factory where the flexible tubing harvested natural sunlight from reflective
       surfaces and transported the light indoors to provide natural daylight for workstations.

•      Installed water curtains paired with ventilation fans at our Hefei and Panyu factories for more energy-efficient temperature control.

•      Conducted regular inspection of our boilers to ensure full compliance with the regulations and allowed timely repair of the boilers.




1
       The increase in energy consumption intensity was mainly driven by the following operational factors:
       – production facility in Panyu: air conditioning systems were installed on two production floors, leading to a planned increase in electricity consumption;
       – production facility in Hefei: energy consumption increased due to the construction of the new logistics facility in the factory compound; and
       – production facility in Thailand: in 2025, the facility underwent a major production system upgrade and employee turnover. During this adaptation phase;
            production volume temporarily decreased, while baseload energy consumption remained stable. As energy consumption intensity is calculated based on
            per standard piece of products, the lower production volume resulted in a significant rise in intensity.

•     Improved the insulation of steam pipes and steam traps which reduced energy loss to the surrounding environment at our Hefei
      factory.

•     Reduced air pressure of air compressors to most energy-efficient level and conducted regular inspection of outlets to ensure
      minimising air pressure leaking.

•     Continued to gradually replace lighting fixtures with LED, aiming at reducing our energy consumption while maintaining sufficient
      lighting for our workers, with our Thailand factory achieving 100% LED lighting.

•     New cooling system installed to additional production floors at our Panyu factory was partly powered by waste steam to enhance the
      energy efficiency.

•     Insulated the feedwater water tank and pipeline in the boiler room and the front cover of the fusing machine to reduce the heat loss at
      our Vietnam factory.

•     Reduced half of the lighting in the passageway to avoid over-lighting at our Vietnam factory.

•     The steam traps of the irons at our Hefei factory had been maintained or renewed to improve the quality of the steam and reduce
      energy loss.

(b) Environmental Data Collection and Monitoring
We collect and monitor material environmental data as part of our standard practice across all facilities. Our Internal Audit Department
verifies data reliability as an internal control measure. This supports more effective decision-making on resource-saving projects, enables
benchmarking our performance against local government requirements to ensure compliance, and addresses customer’s expectation on
resources consumption and emissions control.

(c) Enhancement of Employee Awareness
We promote energy conservation in the workplace by providing clear guidance on energy efficiency to all employees. Through regular
internal communications, we inform our employees about the Group’s latest environmental policies and measures, fostering a culture of
sustainability across operations. We also conduct specific workshops and seminars for the factory management team to further improve our
environmental performance.

7.3 Resources Management
Resource Efficiency
While our garment manufacturing business is subject to the stringent environmental requirements of our customers, the Group is
committed to mitigating its environmental impact by implementing various resources conservation measures and initiatives that promote
efficient consumption of energy, water and other materials across the production chain. For example, we have adopted the Higg Facility
Environmental Module to measure and evaluate the environmental performance of our production facilities in China and Vietnam on
the Worldly platform developed by Cascale (formerly known as “the Sustainable Apparel Coalition”). In addition, in the reporting year,
workshops and seminars were organised by our customers to enhance the knowledge and awareness of our factory management team
regarding environmental protection.

The Group is committed to reducing environmental impact through proper management, and below are resources efficiency related
commitments outlined under the environmental policy in the ESG Policy:

•       Manage internal controls over our significant environmental aspects with the aim to enhance the efficiency of resource use and reduce
        environmental emissions in our business operations;

•       Implement KPIs tracking system for the use of water and electricity as well as disposal of hazardous and non-hazardous waste; and

•       Encourage the use of renewable energy and low-carbon and energy efficient products and materials across our properties and offices.

The Environment and Natural Resources
The significant environmental impact of our garment manufacturing business regarding emissions and the use of resources have been
discussed and disclosed above in this Report.




6
        As the Group’s GHG emissions are mainly derived from Scope 2 (purchased electricity), the increase is therefore attributable to the same operational factors
        mentioned in the energy performance summary above, including construction in Hefei, facility improvements in Panyu, and system upgrades in Thailand.



42     Tristate Holdings Limited Annual Report 2025
Environmental, Social and Governance Report


7.3.1       Use of Water
As our garment manufacturing operations do not involve dyeing and bleaching, major water consumption comes from garment laundry
and steam boilers, along with water use in facility dormitories. We reduce consumption through behavioural changes and equipment
maintenance.

Improvements/Initiatives in the Reporting Year
•   Continued maintenance work with repair and replacement of leaking water inlets and fire service pipes during the reporting year.

Highlighted Initiatives Carried Out in Previous Years
•   Reduced flushing water consumption by retrofitting existing toilets with dual-flush capability and sensor controls.

•      Replaced conventional water fixtures by newer models with low flow regulators.

•      Installed water-softening filters for boilers to improve flow rate and efficiency.

•      Reduced pressure of water pipes in factories to reduce water usage and leakage through the burst of pipes.

•      At our Thailand factory, water system was switched from underground to municipal water where water pipe system was replaced to
       avoid further water leakage from the aged water system of the whole factory.

•      Enhanced control of overtime shifts to better utilise the boilers and reduce both water and energy consumption from nightshifts.

•      More water meters were installed in our Panyu factory for a more integrated data management system in order to monitor water
       consumption.

•      At our Hefei factory, an emergency plan, which had been approved by local government, was set up to deal with incidents that would
       have an impact on the environment and the plan.

•      Increased onsite wastewater data collection and inspection to identify issues in a timely manner and kept updating and improving the
       information board of wastewater discharge point to meet the new municipal regulation.

•      Installed new real-time monitors at the wastewater discharge point to precisely control the pollutants at our Hefei factory.

The Group’s sole water source is municipal water supply. Water consumption is monitored by meters installed at all our production facilities
and offices. Water used by C.P. Company’s showroom in Italy and stores in Italy and France comes from public utilities in shopping malls
or complexes without separate metering. As the consumption volume is not material to the Group, the related water consumption is
excluded from C.P. Company’s water data.

7.3.2          Use of Packaging Materials
For our garment manufacturing business, packaging materials are typically subject to customer specifications, limiting the Group’s
control over the use of materials. Nonetheless, we manage responsible production across our value chain by preventing over-ordering
of packaging materials. For example, we use standard-size carton boxes and polybags to avoid unused stock from size mismatches. We
continued monitoring carton and plastic bags usage in the reporting year, which were the major types of packaging materials we used.

7.3.3          Waste Reduction and Circular Economy
General Waste
The Group continuously optimises production plans and procedures to maximise raw material utilisation and reduce fabric waste
generation. We minimise rag disposal during production by periodically collecting rags for recycling. Our offcut fabric waste recycling
programme operates across our factories in Panyu and Hefei in China and in Thailand and Vietnam. Fabric waste is shredded onsite and
sorted out for recycling, while cartons, paper tubes, and plastics are collected by recyclers.

The ‘3R’ principles – reduce, reuse and recycle – are widely adopted across the Group. General waste from operations, including paper and
household waste, is disposed of in compliance with local requirements and international standards.




7
        The fluctuation in water intensity during the reporting year was largely influenced by the following activities:
        – production facility in Hefei: the increase in water usage was primarily driven by construction work of the new logistics facility; and
        – production facility in Thailand: water consumption increased due to the decreased production efficiency caused by production system update and employee
          turnover.


44     Tristate Holdings Limited Annual Report 2025
Environmental, Social and Governance Report


Hazardous Waste
Our production process involves cutting, sewing, finishing and packaging of garments. Most of the raw materials are purchased from
qualified fabric suppliers designated by our global customers, whose stringent standards minimise hazardous waste generation in
our manufacturing process. Nonetheless, we continually review our production process and will consider setting reduction target as
appropriate.

Common hazardous waste we generate includes old light tubes, empty chemical containers and expired chemicals. To reduce light tube
waste, we progressively replace lighting systems with LED tubes, which are more durable and energy-efficient. For the cleaning reagent,
we used eco-friendly substitutes where practical. Our Chemical Safety Management Policy and the Chemical Risk Assessment and
Management Procedures standardise chemical procurement and disposal procedures across all factories. We engaged qualified third-party
service providers to properly handle and dispose of limited hazardous waste. All disposal activities are documented to ensure compliance
with local laws and regulations on hazardous waste disposal.

 Types of Hazardous Waste                                       Unit                                               2025                        2024
 Light tubes                                                    tonnes                                             0.624                       0.826
 Empty chemical containers                                      tonnes                                             1.559                       1.413
 Expired chemicals                                              tonnes                                             0.179                       0.317

7.4 Emissions Control
Given the nature of our garment manufacturing business, our operations do not generate significant air emissions, hazardous waste, or
wastewater discharge. Nevertheless, the Group strictly adheres to all relevant environmental laws and regulations while continuously
endeavouring to minimise emissions wherever reasonably practicable. To align with the stringent chemical management requirements of
our clients, such as their manufacturing restricted substances lists, we have established comprehensive internal guidelines to ensure that
restricted chemicals are strictly excluded from our production processes.

7.4.1     Air Emissions
Given the nature of our garment manufacturing business, we do not have significant air emissions from our production process. Our
primary air emission sources are boilers for steam production and backup generators.

We have transitioned from diesel boilers to natural gas at our Panyu factory and LPG boiler at our Thailand factory. We appoint external
agencies to regularly assess boiler air emissions and perform timely maintenance when problems are identified.

In the reporting year, air emissions from laser cutting by the new auto-sewing machines were identified at our Panyu and Hefei factories. Air
filters were installed to remove pollutants in the fumes.

7.4.2     Wastewater Discharge
Industrial wastewater generation is limited to laundry operation at our Hefei factory. We operate a dedicated on-site treatment facility with
a dedicated team that employs a tertiary wastewater treatment process, combining sedimentation and biological treatment methods to
ensure all effluent complies with relevant discharge standards.

Apart from meeting local regulatory requirements, we have also adopted the Zero Discharge of Hazardous Chemicals (ZDHC) Wastewater
Guidelines, which were validated through annual testing. We have invested in infrastructure upgrades, including installation of a filter press
at our Hefei water treatment plant to enhance sludge dewatering efficiency. A new data acquisition instrument has been implemented to
ensure wastewater data is completely and accurately uploaded to our online monitoring system. To the best of our knowledge, no material
violation of discharge standards by the Group occurred during the reporting year.

7.5 Regulatory Compliance
During the reporting year, we were not aware of any material non-compliance with laws and regulations relating to environmental
emissions by the Group.

                  (a) the governance body(s) (which can include        Structure

                           individual(s) is informed about climate-

                            into account climate-related risks
                            and opportunities when overseeing
                            the issuer’s strategy, its decisions
                            on major transactions, and its risk

                            or individual(s) has considered trade-
                            offs associated with those risks and
                            opportunities;
                      (iv) how the body(s) or individual(s) oversees

                           paragraphs 37 to 40), including whether

                  (b) management’s role in the governance

                           and procedures to suppor t the

                           are integrated with other internal
                           functions.

     (II) Strategy

     Climate-related risks and opportunities

     Paragraph 20             An issuer shall disclose information to enable              7.2.1                   –
                              an understanding of climate-related risks                   Climate-related Risks
                              and opportunities that could reasonably be                  and Opportunities
                              expected to affect the issuer’s cash flows, its
                              access to finance or cost of capital over the
                              short, medium or long term. Specifically, the
                              issuer shall:

                               (a) describe climate-related risks and
                                   opportunities that could reasonably be
                                   expected to affect the issuer’s cash flows,
                                   its access to finance or cost of capital over
                                   the short, medium or long term;

                               (b) explain, for each climate-related risk
                                   the issuer has identified, whether the
                                   issuer considers the risk to be a climate-
                                   related physical risk or climate-related
                                   transition risk ;

                               (c) specify, for each climate-related risk and
                                   opportunity the issuer has identified,
                                   over which time horizons – short, medium
                                   or long term – the effects of each climate-
                                   related risk and opportunity could
                                   reasonably be expected to occur; and

                               (d) explain how the issuer defines ‘short term’,
                                   ‘medium term’ and ‘long term’ and how
                                   these definitions are linked to the planning
                                   horizons used by the issuer for strategic
                                   decision-making.

                              and opportunities on the issuer’s business
                              model and value chain . Specifically, the
                              issuer shall disclose:

                               (a) a d e s c r i p t i o n o f t h e c u r re n t a n d
                                   anticipated effects of climate-related
                                   risks and opportunities on the issuer’s
                                   business model and value chain ; and

                               (b) a description of where in the issuer’s
                                   business model and value chain climate-
                                   related risks and opportunities are
                                   concentrated (for example, geographical
                                   areas, facilities and types of assets).

     (III) Risk Management

     Paragraph 27              An issuer shall disclose information about:         7.2.1                   –
                                                                                   Climate-related Risks
                               (a) the processes and related policies it uses      and Opportunities
                                   to identify, assess, prioritise and monitor
                                   climate-related risks, including information
                                   about:

                                    (i) the inputs and parameters the
                                        issuer uses (for example, information
                                        about data sources and the scope of
                                        operations covered in the processes);

                                    (ii) whether and how the issuer uses
                                         climate-related scenario analysis to
                                         inform its identification of climate-
                                         related risks;

                                    (iii) how the issuer assesses the nature,
                                          likelihood and magnitude of the
                                          effects of those risks (for example,
                                          whether the issuer considers
                                          qualitative factors, quantitative
                                          thresholds or other criteria);

                                    (iv) whether and how the issuer prioritises
                                         climate-related risks relative to other
                                         types of risks;

                                    (v) how the issuer monitors climate-
                                        related risks; and

                                    (vi) whether and how the issuer has
                                         changed the processes it uses
                                         compared with the previous reporting
                                         period;

                               (b) the processes the issuer uses to identify,
                                   assess, prioritise and monitor climate-
                                   related oppor tunities (including
                                   information about whether and how
                                   the issuer uses climate-related scenario
                                   analysis to inform its identification of
                                   climate-related opportunities); and

                               (c) the extent to which, and how, the
                                   processes for identifying, assessing,
                                   prioritising and monitoring climate-
                                   related risks and opportunities are
                                   integrated into and inform the issuer’s
                                   overall risk management process.

                                   applies to the issuer in its entirety or only
                                   a part of the issuer, such as a specific
                                   business unit or geographic region);

                               (d) the period over which the target applies;

                               (e) the base period from which progress is
                                   measured;

                               (f ) milestones or interim targets (if any);

                               (g) if the target is quantitative, whether the
                                   target is an absolute target or an intensity
                                   target; and

                               (h) how the latest international agreement
                                   on climate change , including jurisdictional
                                   commitments that arise from that
                                   agreement, has informed the target.

Share Options
The share option scheme of the Company (the “2016 Share Option Scheme”) was adopted by the shareholders at the annual general
meeting of the Company held on 6 June 2016 for granting options to eligible persons to subscribe for shares of the Company.

The principal terms of the 2016 Share Option Scheme are summarised below:

Purpose                                           :   To provide participants with the opportunity to acquire proprietary interests in
                                                      the Company, to encourage participants to work towards achieving the relevant
                                                      performance targets in order to enhance the value of the Company and its shares
                                                      for the benefit of the Company and its shareholders as a whole, and to retain
                                                      participants who achieve such performance targets.

                                                      To provide the Company with a flexible means of either retaining, incentivising,
                                                      rewarding, remunerating, compensating and/or providing benefits to
                                                      participants.

Participants                                      :   The directors, officers and/or employees of any member of the Group as
                                                      determined by the Board from time to time.

Total number of shares available for issue and    :   The maximum number of shares which may be issued upon exercise of all
  the percentage of the issued share capital          options granted and to be granted under the 2016 Share Option Scheme shall
  that it represents at the date of this report       not in aggregate exceed 10% of the total number of shares in issue (excluding
                                                      treasury shares, if any) at the date of the adoption of the 2016 Share Option
                                                      Scheme (i.e. 27,160,725 shares). At the date of this report, the total number of
                                                      shares available for issue in respect of all options granted (but not yet exercised)
                                                      and options to be granted under the 2016 Share Option Scheme is 25,289,725
                                                      shares, representing approximately 9.25% of the issued share capital of the
                                                      Company (excluding treasury shares, if any).

Maximum entitlement of each participant           :   The total number of shares issued and to be issued upon exercise of options
                                                      granted to each participant (including exercised, cancelled and outstanding
                                                      options) in any 12-month period shall not exceed 1% of the shares in issue
                                                      (excluding treasury shares, if any).

Period within which the shares must be taken      :   Such period as specified by the Board at the time of grant which, however, shall
  up under an option                                  not expire later than ten years from the relevant date of grant.

Minimum period for which an option must be        :   Any minimum period as specified by the Board at the time of grant whereas the
  held before it can be exercised                     2016 Share Option Scheme does not specify any minimum period.

Amount payable on acceptance of the option        :   HK$1.00 (or an equivalent amount) to be paid as consideration upon acceptance
 and the period within which payment must             of the option which is open for ten business days for acceptance from the date of
 be made                                              offer.

Basis of determining the subscription price       :   The subscription price shall be such price determined by the Board at its absolute
                                                      discretion which shall be no less than the higher of (i) the closing price of the
                                                      shares as stated in the daily quotations sheet issued by The Stock Exchange of
                                                      Hong Kong Limited (the “Stock Exchange”) on the date of grant; (ii) the average
                                                      closing price of the shares as stated in the daily quotations sheets issued by the
                                                      Stock Exchange for the five business days immediately preceding the date of
                                                      grant; and (iii) the nominal value of a share on the date of grant.

Remaining life of the 2016 Share Option           :   The 2016 Share Option Scheme shall be valid and effective for a period of ten
  Scheme                                              years from the date of its adoption (i.e. to expire on 6 June 2026).
